> o Measure performance through citations, impact factors, and international academic databases.

• Curriculum Internationalisation

> o Integrate internationally relevant pedagogies, case studies, and content into existing curricula.
>
> o Encourage co-creation of syllabi with international academic partners.

• Promotion of Global Literature and Texts

> o Translate renowned international texts to enrich local learning resources.
>
> o Disseminate culturally significant works to broaden global understanding.

• Student-Centred Joint Ventures

> o Maintain continuity of joint programs and initiatives focused on student mobility, mentorship, and learning.
>
> o Strengthen support systems for international learning pathways.

• International Cultural Exchange and Outreach

> o Organize cross-cultural events with visiting delegations and student exchange participants.
>
> o Facilitate staff and student visits abroad to foster global understanding.

• Review and Quality Assurance Mechanisms

> o Implement periodic review systems to evaluate mobility schemes and partnership outcomes.
>
> o Train administrative and academic staff to monitor and improve the quality of international engagements.

**34.21. International Collaboration – Long-term Goals**

• Institutional Policy and Governance Framework

> o Promote a comprehensive and holistic internationalization policy embedded in institutional governance.
>
> o Foster a long-term vision aligned with global best practices in higher education internationalization.

• Institutional Culture of Internationalization

> o Create a vibrant, inclusive environment that embraces internationalization across academics, research, and administration.
>
> o Integrate global engagement into the ethos of the University and its constituent units.

• Integrated Institutional Relations System

> o Build strong coordination between the central institution and its constituent units.
>
> o Develop a unified, effective, and responsive Institutional Relations (IR) framework.

• Strategic Global Partnerships

> o Diversify and strengthen academic linkages with high-ranking FHEIs.
>
> o Leverage institutional strengths to expand collaborative research, exchanges, and innovation networks.

• Global Academic Excellence

> o Promote joint knowledge creation, research, and exchange of academic, technological, and cultural resources with global partners.
>
> o Encourage co-authored publications, dual degree initiatives, and interdisciplinary research.

• Preferred Destination for International Students

> o Position the institution as a global education hub by highlighting unique programs, eminent faculty, and strong research infrastructure.
>
> o Enhance international student services for smooth integration and support.

• Sustainable Internationalization Efforts

> o Ensure continuity and relevance of international initiatives through long-term planning.
>
> o Align institutional priorities with the evolving global educational landscape.

• Expanded Academic and Research Exchange

> o Increase outbound and inbound exchange opportunities to meet global learning and research standards.
>
> o Encourage cross-border academic mobility and partnerships.

• Capacity Building for International Engagement

> o Equip faculty and staff with global competencies through training and exposure.
>
> o Foster leadership in international academic strategy and cross-cultural communication.

• Enhancement of Institutional Rankings and Recognition

> o Improve international rankings by strengthening global collaborations and academic output.
>
> o Measure progress through metrics like citations, global visibility, and institutional reputation.

• Curriculum and Pedagogy Internationalization

> o Diversify curricula and teaching methodologies to align with global standards.
>
> o Encourage international benchmarking and curriculum co-development.

• Ethos of International Studies and Collaboration

> o Develop centres or programs focused on international studies and global challenges.
>
> o Promote scholarly exchange and research in international relations and crosscultural studies.

• Faculty Empowerment for Global Standards

> o Provide training and opportunities to align faculty competencies with international academic standards.
>
> o Encourage global teaching, research collaborations, and leadership roles in academic networks.

• Cultural Understanding and Inclusivity

> o Deepen awareness of global cultures and promote respect for diversity.
>
> o Integrate intercultural learning into student life and campus programming.

• Global Academic and Cultural Leadership

> o Position the institution as a leader in academic excellence, cultural understanding, and innovation in research.
>
> o Align long-term vision with global benchmarks for excellence and inclusion.

**35. Physical Enablers**

**35.1. Innovation and Entrepreneurship – Smart Campus**

1\. Integrated Technology Infrastructure

• Implement IoT-enabled systems for real-time monitoring and automation of:

> o Energy usage
>
> o Water management
>
> o Security and access control
>
> o Classroom utilities and occupancy

2\. Cost-Effective Resource Management

• **Use data analytics to optimize:**

> o Operational costs
>
> o Maintenance schedules
>
> o Utility consumption patterns

• Enable predictive maintenance to reduce downtime and costs.

3\. Enhanced Comfort and User Experience

• Ensure climate-responsive and adaptive facilities for students, faculty, and visitors.

• Incorporate smart lighting, air quality control, and digital navigation across campus.

4\. Risk Reduction and Emergency Response

• Deploy sensors and alert systems for fire, intrusions, or environmental hazards.

• Integrate automated emergency protocols and evacuation systems.

5\. Resilience and Sustainability

• **Design infrastructure that is:**

> o Disaster-resilient and climate-conscious
>
> o Capable of operating independently during crises (e.g., solar backup, offline servers)
>
> o Built with smart materials and sustainable practices

**35.2. Innovation and Entrepreneurship – Smart Campus: Proactive Problem Detection**

1\. Real-Time Monitoring Systems

• **Deploy IoT sensors and smart meters to detect anomalies in:**

> o Electricity and water supply
>
> o HVAC and lighting systems
>
> o Building occupancy and structural health

2\. Predictive Maintenance

• **Use AI and machine learning algorithms to:**

> o Predict equipment failures
>
> o Schedule maintenance before breakdowns
>
> o Reduce downtime and repair costs

3\. Seamless User Experience

• **Prevent disruptions that impact:**

> o Academic activities (e.g., projector or Wi-Fi outages)
>
> o Safety and accessibility (e.g., elevator issues, blocked pathways)
>
> o Comfort (e.g., lighting or air conditioning faults)

4\. Automated Alerts and Escalation

• Enable real-time alerts to maintenance teams and administrators.

• Integrate ticketing systems to track issue resolution timelines.

5\. Student and Staff Well-being

• **Foster a campus environment that is:**

> o Efficient, quiet, and functional
>
> o Free from unnecessary distractions
>
> o Conducive to learning, teaching, and research

**35.3. Innovation and Entrepreneurship – Smart Campus: Performance Infrastructure**

1\. Interconnected Building Systems

• **Establish interoperable systems where:**

> o Lighting, HVAC, security, and energy systems communicate and coordinate
>
> o All systems are integrated through a centralized Building Management System (BMS)

2\. Automated and Adaptive Controls

• **Enable automatic adjustments based on:**

> o Occupancy levels
>
> o Time of day or academic schedule
>
> o Environmental conditions (temperature, humidity, light)

3\. Common Outcomes Optimization

• **Coordinate systems to achieve shared objectives such as:**

> o Energy efficiency and cost savings
>
> o User comfort in classrooms, labs, and offices
>
> o Enhanced safety through synchronized surveillance and access control

4\. Central Monitoring and Analytics

• **Use a smart campus dashboard to:**

> o Monitor performance metrics in real-time
>
> o Analyze usage patterns and generate insights
>
> o Enable data-driven decisions for facilities management

5\. Sustainable and Scalable Design

• **Build a flexible infrastructure that supports:**

> o Scalability for new technologies and facilities
>
> o Sustainability goals like reduced carbon footprint and smart energy use

**35.4. Innovation and Entrepreneurship – Smart Campus: Operational Excellence and Sustainability**

1\. Facility Uptime and On-Demand Performance

• Ensure maximum availability and reliability of key infrastructure and utilities.

• Enable on-demand control of building functions (lighting, HVAC, IT services) based on usage and schedules.

2\. Energy Efficiency and Environmental Targets

• Implement strategies to meet greenhouse gas (GHG) reduction goals, including:

> o Smart energy meters and consumption analytics
>
> o LED lighting, motion sensors, and automated shutdown systems
>
> o Renewable energy integration (solar, wind, geothermal)

3\. Cost and Risk Mitigation

• **Protect against variable energy pricing through:**

> o Demand-side energy management
>
> o Battery storage systems and grid optimization
>
> o Energy purchasing strategies and forecasting models

4\. Adoption of Emerging Technologies

• **Embrace next-gen technologies such as:**

> o Artificial Intelligence (AI) and Machine Learning (ML) for energy prediction
>
> o Blockchain for utility billing and resource tracking
>
> o Smart sensors and IoT for environmental monitoring

5\. Sustainability Reporting and Compliance

• **Track and report performance against:**

> o Sustainability standards (e.g., GRI, ISO 50001)
>
> o Institutional carbon neutrality goals
>
> o National Green Campus initiatives

**35.5. Innovation and Entrepreneurship – Green/Sustainable Building: Basic Requirements**

1\. Energy Efficiency

• Use energy-efficient materials and systems, including:

> o LED lighting and motion sensors
>
> o Energy-efficient HVAC systems
>
> o Smart metering and automated controls

• Integrate renewable energy sources (e.g., solar panels, wind turbines)

2\. Water Conservation

• Install low-flow fixtures and dual-flush toilets

• Implement rainwater harvesting systems

• Use greywater recycling for landscape irrigation and flushing

3\. Sustainable Materials

• Prioritize locally sourced, recycled, and low-carbon materials

• Use non-toxic, durable, and eco-certified construction components

4\. Indoor Environmental Quality

• Ensure adequate natural lighting and ventilation

• Use low-VOC paints and finishes to maintain air quality

• Incorporate thermal and acoustic insulation for comfort

5\. Waste Management

• Plan for construction waste segregation and recycling

• Design spaces for ongoing waste reduction and recycling infrastructure

6\. Landscaping and Biodiversity

• Use native and drought-resistant plants

• Minimize paved areas and maximize green cover

• Create green roofs, bio-walls, and eco-corridors

7\. Certifications and Standards

• **Aim for certification under:**

> o GRIHA, IGBC, or LEED rating systems
>
> o ISO 14001 for environmental management

**35.6. Innovation and Entrepreneurship – Green/Sustainable Building: Open Environment Principle**

1\. Open Environment Design Philosophy

• Promote natural interaction between indoor and outdoor spaces

• Maximize daylighting, ventilation, and visual connectivity with nature

• Use open courtyards, atriums, and shaded walkways to reduce energy load

2\. Optimized Energy Consumption

• Employ passive design techniques (orientation, shading, thermal mass)

• Use smart systems for energy monitoring, scheduling, and automation

• Incorporate renewable energy sources like solar, wind, and geothermal

3\. Efficient Water Management

• Install rainwater harvesting and greywater reuse systems

• Use smart irrigation and low-flow fixtures

• Incorporate permeable surfaces and bioswales to support groundwater recharge

4\. Adaptive and Sustainable Building Envelope

• Utilize high-performance glazing, green roofs, and natural insulation

• Reduce reliance on mechanical systems through climate-responsive architecture

5\. Environmental Modeling and Planning

• Use simulation tools for water, energy, and thermal performance

• Design based on climatic zone-specific green building models

**35.7. Innovation and Entrepreneurship – Green/Sustainable Building: Internal Sustainable Operations**

1\. Green Energy Utilization

• **Generate and use renewable energy sources such as:**

> o Solar power systems (rooftop PV panels, solar thermal heaters)
>
> o Wind turbines or biogas plants for supplementary energy

• Integrate with smart grids and energy storage systems

2\. Harvested and Recycled Water

• Implement rainwater harvesting and groundwater recharge systems

• Use greywater recycling for non-potable uses (irrigation, flushing)

• Monitor usage with smart water meters and leak detection systems

3\. Sustainable Resource Management

• Utilize recycled materials for construction, interiors, and furnishings

• Promote paperless systems and digital workflows

• Implement waste segregation, composting, and upcycling programs

4\. Self-Sustaining Inputs

• **Design systems that sustainably provide:**

> o Air – through natural ventilation, green walls, and pollution filters
>
> o Water – via rainwater storage and purification units
>
> o Food – with on-campus organic gardens or community farming spaces
>
> o Light – using daylight-optimized architecture and solar lighting
>
> o Electricity – with renewable energy and battery backup systems

5\. Monitoring and Performance Optimization

• Use IoT-based dashboards to track resource generation and consumption

• Continuously improve performance based on efficiency data and sustainability goals

**35.8. Green/Sustainable Building – Aspirational Requirements (High-Tech, Clean-Green Concept)**

1\. Centralized Smart HVAC Systems

• Use energy-efficient, centralized air conditioning with:

> o Variable Refrigerant Flow (VRF) or chiller-based cooling
>
> o Smart zoning for room-specific climate control
>
> o Heat recovery systems to minimize energy waste

2\. High-Tech Building Automation

• Integrate a Building Management System (BMS) for:

> o Real-time monitoring and optimization of energy, lighting, and HVAC
>
> o Automatic fault detection and response
>
> o Adaptive performance based on occupancy and time-of-day

3\. Clean-Green Environmental Design

• **Incorporate:**

> o Air purification and CO₂ control systems
>
> o Indoor plants and biophilic elements
>
> o High-performance glazing and shading systems to reduce solar gain

4\. Sustainable Energy and Materials

• Ensure solar integration for HVAC support and lighting backup

• Use eco-certified materials (low VOCs, recycled content, sustainable sourcing)

• Install smart meters and energy dashboards for transparency

5\. Water and Waste Efficiency

• **Include:**

> o Sensor-based faucets and water-saving fixtures
>
> o Rainwater harvesting systems for HVAC cooling towers
>
> o Green rooftops for insulation and stormwater management

**35.9. Infrastructure to Commute – Inclusive and Accessible Campus Mobility**

1\. Pedestrian-Friendly Pathways

• Develop wide, paved, and well-marked walkways across campus.

• Include anti-slip surfaces, tactile paving, and ramp access at all critical points.

2\. Differently-Abled Accessibility

• Provide separate, barrier-free lanes for wheelchair users.

• Install handrails, gentle slopes, and automatic door systems in all major buildings.

• Ensure elevators are accessible with Braille and audio support.

3\. Street Signs and Wayfinding

• **Install clear signage with:**

> o Bilingual and visually contrasting fonts
>
> o Symbols and arrows for quick recognition
>
> o Directional maps at strategic locations

4\. Smart Transport and Commute Options

• Offer electric shuttle services or golf carts for elderly and disabled individuals.

• Create designated parking areas and drop-off zones for easy campus access.

5\. Safety and Lighting

• Ensure well-lit paths, crosswalks, and speed-calming measures.

• Include motion-sensor lighting and emergency call stations along commuting routes.

**35.10. Infrastructure to Commute – Bicycle & Battery-Powered Vehicle Access**

1\. Dedicated Bicycle Lanes

• Develop safe, wide, and continuous bicycle paths across the campus.

• Ensure clearly marked lanes, reflective signs, and traffic separation from motorized vehicles.

2\. Battery-Powered Vehicle Integration

• Allow and support use of e-scooters, e-bikes, and small electric shuttles.

• Provide charging stations and designated parking zones for battery-operated vehicles.

3\. High-Quality Motorways

• **Maintain well-paved internal roads designed for:**

> o Smooth vehicle flow
>
> o Low-speed safety zones near academic and pedestrian areas
>
> o Separate lanes for service and emergency vehicles

4\. Parking and Storage Facilities

• Construct covered bicycle stands and secure docking stations.

• Offer charging-enabled parking lots for electric vehicles with surveillance.

5\. Promotion and Awareness

• Run campaigns to promote eco-friendly commuting among students and staff.

• Provide incentives for using bicycles and electric vehicles (e.g., priority parking, rental programs).

**35.11. Infrastructure to Commute – Accessibility for PwD**

1\. Barrier-Free Pathways

• Design smooth, wide, and obstacle-free walkways across campus.

• Ensure non-slip surfaces and tactile paving for visually impaired individuals.

2\. Ramps and Lifts

• Install gentle-slope ramps with handrails at all building entrances.

• **Provide elevators with:**

> o Braille buttons
>
> o Voice announcements
>
> o Sufficient space for wheelchairs

3\. Reserved Transport Services

• **Offer dedicated transport options such as:**

> o Low-floor electric shuttles
>
> o Priority pick-up zones for wheelchair users

4\. Accessible Parking and Drop-Off

• Create reserved parking spots near building entrances.

• Designate drop-off zones with covered waiting areas and seating.

5\. Wayfinding and Signage

• Use high-contrast, large-font signs with Braille and directional arrows.

• Install audio-guided kiosks and digital maps at key intersections.

6\. Emergency and Safety Provisions

• Include accessible emergency call points and evacuation plans for PwD.

• Train staff and security in disability-inclusive safety protocols.

**35.12. Infrastructure – Administrative Block (Admission & Counselling Area)**

1\. Adequate and Functional Space

• Design spacious, well-ventilated rooms for:

> o Admission processing
>
> o Student counselling
>
> o Document verification and storage
>
> o Waiting and helpdesk areas

2\. Student-Friendly Layout

• Provide clear signage and guided pathways for smooth navigation.

• Include separate counters for admission, counselling, scholarships, and grievance redressal.

3\. Comfortable Waiting Zones

• **Equip with:**

> o Seating arrangements for students and parents
>
> o Drinking water and restrooms
>
> o Digital displays for real-time updates and announcements

4\. Confidential Counselling Rooms

• **Ensure private and soundproof spaces for:**

> o Academic guidance
>
> o Career counselling
>
> o Mental health support

5\. Digital and Physical Infrastructure

• **Integrate:**

> o Computerized admission systems and databases
>
> o Secure document storage (digital and hard copy)
>
> o High-speed internet and printing/scanning facilities

**35.13. Infrastructure – Administrative Block: Faculty Cubicles (Desirable Requirements)**

1\. Adequate Number of Cubicles

• Provide sufficient cubicles to accommodate all faculty engaged in:

> o Admission counselling
>
> o Academic advising
>
> o Departmental administration and research support

2\. Functional Design

• **Ensure each cubicle includes:**

> o Ergonomic furniture (desk, chair, storage)
>
> o Computer and power outlets
>
> o Adequate lighting and ventilation

3\. Privacy and Comfort

• Design with partitions for semi-privacy, enabling:

> o One-on-one student interaction
>
> o Confidential academic discussions
>
> o Minimal distractions during administrative tasks

4\. Accessibility and Placement

• **Position cubicles near:**

> o Admission and counselling areas for easy access
>
> o Department heads and support staff for coordination
>
> o Meeting or conference rooms for group advising

5\. Integration with Digital Systems

• **Equip with:**

> o Institutional email and information systems
>
> o Student data portals and admission software
>
> o Internet access and telecommunication tools

**35.14. Infrastructure – Administrative Block: Department-Specific Buildings**

1\. Independent Departmental Buildings

• Allow academic departments to operate from separate, dedicated buildings.

• **Ensure each building supports:**

> o Department-specific administrative tasks
>
> o Faculty offices and meeting rooms
>
> o Labs, classrooms, and research spaces

2\. Decentralized but Connected Layout

• **Design a distributed campus layout where:**

> o Departments function autonomously
>
> o Still remain digitally and logistically connected to the central administrative block

3\. Department-Level Admission and Counselling

• **Provide space within each building for:**

> o Course-specific admissions counselling
>
> o Faculty-student academic advising
>
> o Departmental coordination offices

4\. Efficient Signage and Wayfinding

• Install clear directional signs and campus maps to help students and visitors navigate to respective departments.

5\. Infrastructure Standards Across Departments

• **Ensure uniform access to:**

> o IT infrastructure and internet
>
> o Student support services
>
> o Accessibility and safety features

**35.15. Infrastructure – Library / Digital Resource Centre**

1\. Adequate Space Allocation

• **Provide separate zones for:**

> o Reading rooms with silent and group study areas
>
> o Book and journal stock rooms
>
> o Reference and digital access terminals

2\. Reading Room Facilities

• **Ensure:**

> o Comfortable seating with adequate lighting and ventilation
>
> o Extended working hours during exams and project deadlines
>
> o Accessible spaces for students with disabilities

3\. Physical Collection Management

• **Organize stock areas with:**

> o Open shelves and catalogue systems
>
> o Archival storage for rare books and journals
>
> o Barcode/RFID-enabled inventory tracking

4\. Digital Resource Integration

• **Provide access to:**

> o E-books, e-journals, and online databases (e.g., JSTOR, IEEE, ScienceDirect)
>
> o Learning Management Systems (LMS)
>
> o Digital archives and institutional repositories

5\. Technology and Support Services

• **Equip with:**

> o High-speed internet, computers, and printers
>
> o Digital cataloguing systems (e.g., KOHA, Libsys)
>
> o Assistance counters for research help and digital literacy

**35.16. Infrastructure – Lecture Complex, Classrooms, and Learning Spaces**

1\. Variety of Room Types

• **Ensure availability of:**

> o Large lecture halls for mass instruction
>
> o Medium-sized classrooms for standard courses
>
> o Small tutorial rooms for group learning
>
> o Discussion/seminar rooms for collaborative engagement

2\. Comfortable and Ergonomic Seating

• **Provide:**

> o Tiered seating in large lecture halls for better visibility
>
> o Movable chairs and tables in tutorial/discussion rooms for flexibility
>
> o Adequate legroom, ventilation, and lighting in all rooms

3\. Teaching-Learning Technologies

• **Equip rooms with:**

> o Digital projectors and smart boards
>
> o High-quality audio systems and microphones
>
> o Wi-Fi connectivity and power outlets for devices
>
> o Lecture recording/streaming setups in major halls

4\. Accessibility and Safety

• **Ensure:**

> o Barrier-free access to all rooms for students with disabilities
>
> o Emergency exits, fire safety equipment, and ventilation systems

5\. Supportive Infrastructure

• **Provide:**

> o Timetable coordination systems for efficient room usage
>
> o Whiteboards, podiums, and digital teaching aids
>
> o Ambient temperature control (fans/AC as required)

**35.17. Tutorial Rooms – Integration of Video Recording Facilities**

1\. Purpose and Use Cases

• Enable recording of tutorials, workshops, and interactive sessions for:

> o Asynchronous learning
>
> o Revision and exam preparation
>
> o Flipped classroom models

2\. Recording Equipment Setup

• **Install:**

> o High-definition cameras (static or PTZ – pan/tilt/zoom)
>
> o Boundary or lapel microphones for clear audio capture
>
> o Digital whiteboard capture tools or screen mirroring systems

3\. Control and Storage Systems

• **Use:**

> o Recording consoles or media hubs for easy management
>
> o Automated recording and uploading systems linked to LMS or cloud storage
>
> o Secure file storage with role-based access for privacy

4\. Room Design Considerations

• **Ensure:**

> o Soundproofing to reduce ambient noise
>
> o Proper lighting (diffused overhead and front lighting)
>
> o Unobstructed camera views of teaching area and whiteboards

5\. Integration with Teaching Tools

• **Sync with:**

> o Smart boards, visualizers, and digital displays
>
> o Live-streaming tools (e.g., Zoom, MS Teams, Google Meet)
>
> o LMS platforms (e.g., Moodle, Canvas) for resource sharing

**35.18. Infrastructure – Examination Branch**

1\. Dedicated Examination Branch

• Allocate a separate administrative area exclusively for examination-related operations.

• Ensure restricted access to authorized personnel only.

2\. Strong Room for Confidential Material

• **Construct a secure strong room with:**

> o Thick reinforced walls and metal doors
>
> o Surveillance cameras and alarm systems
>
> o Access logs or biometric entry systems

3\. Capacity and Layout

• Ensure space is adequate to store bulk confidential documents, such as:

> o Question papers
>
> o Answer scripts
>
> o Sealed exam packs and evaluation records

4\. Environmental Control

• **Maintain:**

> o Climate-controlled settings (humidity and temperature)
>
> o Fireproof cabinets or safes
>
> o Pest control measures to protect paper-based materials

5\. Operational Zones

• **Include dedicated areas for:**

> o Printing and sealing of question papers
>
> o Sorting and packaging of exam materials
>
> o Dispatch and receipt log counters

**35.19. Facilities to Faculty and Staff – Faculty Chambers**

1\. Adequate Number of Chambers

• **Allocate sufficient chambers to accommodate:**

> o Permanent faculty members
>
> o Visiting and adjunct faculty
>
> o Part-time instructors and teaching assistants
>
> o Research scholars and postdoctoral fellows

2\. Functional Infrastructure

• **Each chamber should include:**

> o Ergonomic desk and chair setup
>
> o Computer systems with internet access
>
> o Storage cabinets and bookshelves
>
> o Whiteboard or noticeboard for academic use

3\. Privacy and Comfort

• **Ensure:**

> o Semi-private or private spaces for focused academic work
>
> o Sound insulation for meetings and consultations
>
> o Natural lighting and proper ventilation

4\. Connectivity and Utilities

• **Provide:**

> o High-speed internet and LAN ports
>
> o Uninterrupted power supply
>
> o Telephonic intercom systems for internal communication

5\. Accessibility and Proximity

• **Position chambers:**

> o Close to classrooms, departmental offices, and meeting rooms
>
> o Accessible to students for academic interactions and mentoring

**35.20. Facilities to Faculty and Staff – Residential Quarters (Desirable Requirements)**

1\. On-Campus Residential Quarters

• Develop 2–3 bedroom apartments or quarters for:

> o Resident faculty members
>
> o Administrative staff and senior researchers
>
> o Visiting professors on long-term stay

2\. Housing Configuration

• **Design quarters with:**

> o Living room, kitchen, and dining space
>
> o Bedrooms with attached bathrooms
>
> o Basic furnishings and utilities (fans, lighting, wardrobes)

3\. Comfort and Livability

• **Ensure:**

> o Ventilation, natural light, and privacy
>
> o Air-conditioning (where applicable)
>
> o 24/7 water and power supply

4\. Community and Family-Friendly

• **Include:**

> o Children’s play area or green zones nearby
>
> o Common areas for faculty gatherings or recreation
>
> o Proximity to school/daycare if available on campus

5\. Safety and Accessibility

• **Provide:**

> o CCTV surveillance and gated security
>
> o Emergency medical support within reach
>
> o Wheelchair-accessible entrances and facilities

**35.21. Infrastructure – Meeting Rooms**

1\. Space and Layout

• **Ensure meeting rooms are:**

> o Adequately sized based on seating capacity norms (e.g., 10, 20, 30+ persons)
>
> o Easily accessible from faculty chambers and administrative blocks
>
> o Designed for formal, collaborative, and strategic meetings

2\. Furniture and Comfort

• **Provide:**

> o Conference tables and ergonomic chairs
>
> o Side tables or podiums for presentation materials
>
> o Acoustic treatment for clear communication and sound insulation

3\. Electronic Communication Tools

• **Equip with:**

> o High-speed internet and LAN ports
>
> o Teleconferencing systems (mic, speaker, camera)
>
> o Wireless screen sharing tools (e.g., Chromecast, Apple TV)

4\. Presentation and Recording Equipment

• **Include:**

> o Digital whiteboards or traditional whiteboards
>
> o Document cameras or visualizers
>
> o Video recording and streaming capabilities (for meetings, reviews, workshops)

5\. Support Utilities

• **Ensure:**

> o Adequate power sockets and charging points
>
> o Lighting controls and blinds for presentations
>
> o Ventilation and air conditioning

**35.22. Infrastructure – Office Rooms Suitable for All Staff Members**

1\. Space Planning

• **Allocate sufficient number of office rooms to accommodate:**

> o Administrative staff
>
> o Department coordinators and support personnel
>
> o Technical and finance teams
>
> o HR and admissions personnel

2\. Functional Layout

• **Each office room should include:**

> o Workstations or desks with drawers and writing space
>
> o Chairs with ergonomic design for long working hours
>
> o Shared storage cabinets or personal lockers

3\. Work Efficiency Features

• **Provide:**

> o High-speed internet and telephone connections
>
> o Printers, scanners, and office automation tools
>
> o Sufficient lighting, ventilation, and AC/fans

4\. Accessibility and Comfort

• **Ensure:**

> o Proximity to meeting rooms and departmental heads
>
> o Accessible design for staff with disabilities
>
> o Break zones or common lounges nearby for relaxation

5\. Security and Maintenance

• **Equip with:**

> o Secure locking systems for confidential materials
>
> o Cleaning and maintenance support
>
> o Fire safety systems and emergency exits

**35.23. Laboratories and Research Centres – Basic Requirements**

1\. Diverse and Specialized Facilities

• **Establish modern laboratories across core disciplines:**

> o Physical sciences, life sciences, computer science, engineering, etc.

• **Include super-specialty research centres for:**

> o Interdisciplinary innovation (e.g., nanotech, AI, biotechnology, materials science)

2\. State-of-the-Art Equipment

• **Equip with:**

> o Precision instruments and sensors
>
> o Advanced computational tools and simulation software
>
> o Clean rooms and controlled environments for sensitive experiments

3\. Infrastructure and Safety

• **Ensure:**

> o Uninterrupted power supply and backup systems
>
> o Ventilation, fume hoods, and exhaust systems where applicable
>
> o Fire safety, first-aid kits, and emergency protocols

4\. Digital Integration

• **Integrate:**

> o High-speed internet and cloud storage for data processing and backups
>
> o Lab management software for tracking inventory, experiments, and results
>
> o Smart monitoring systems for equipment usage and lab safety

5\. Collaborative Environment

• **Design for:**

> o Team-based research and experimentation
>
> o Dedicated spaces for doctoral/postdoctoral researchers
>
> o Access to industry-standard tools and co-working areas

**35.24. Laboratories and Research Centres – Departmental Libraries (Desirable Requirements)**

1\. Departmental Library Setup

• **Establish mini-libraries within each department to support:**

> o Lab-based research
>
> o Project reference
>
> o Faculty and scholar access to domain-specific resources

2\. Physical Resource Collection

• **Maintain a curated selection of:**

> o Reference books, journals, and manuals
>
> o Technical standards and case studies
>
> o Theses and dissertations from past students

3\. Digital Access Infrastructure

• **Provide:**

> o Computers or digital kiosks with access to institutional e-resources
>
> o Wi-Fi-enabled reading spaces for online literature searches
>
> o Subscription-based access to digital databases (IEEE, Scopus, Springer, etc.)

4\. Resource Integration

• **Link departmental libraries with:**

> o Central library catalogue for unified access
>
> o LMS (Learning Management Systems) for seamless resource sharing
>
> o Remote access portals for off-campus usage

5\. User-Friendly Environment

• **Include:**

> o Comfortable reading zones and discussion desks
>
> o Photocopying, printing, and scanning facilities
>
> o Extended hours during exam and research deadlines

**35.25. Computer Centre / Multimedia Studios – Basic Requirements**

1\. Computer-Student Ratio Compliance

• **Maintain a Computer:**Student ratio as per regulatory norms (e.g., 1:6 or better)

• Ensure availability during all working hours, with extended access during exams/projects

2\. Hardware and Software Requirements

• **Equip with:**

> o Latest generation desktops/laptops with high-speed processors
>
> o Monitors with ergonomic and accessible design
>
> o Licensed software for programming, data analysis, design, and editing
>
> o Multimedia tools for content creation and AV production

3\. Internet and Network Infrastructure

• **Provide:**

> o High-speed wired and wireless internet connectivity
>
> o LAN for resource sharing and remote access
>
> o Firewall and cybersecurity protocols

4\. Facilities and Layout

• **Include:**

> o Adequate number of workstations with comfortable seating
>
> o Air conditioning and dust-free environment
>
> o UPS and power backup systems for uninterrupted operation

5\. Technical and Operational Support

• **Employ:**

> o Trained technical staff for hardware/software maintenance
>
> o Helpdesk or ticketing system for issue resolution
>
> o User training sessions for new tools and platforms

**35.26. Multimedia Studios – Aspirational Requirements**

1\. Digital Content Creation Facilities

• **Set up dedicated multimedia studios to support:**

> o Video lectures and tutorial recording
>
> o MOOCs, webinars, and online course development
>
> o Interactive content creation (AR/VR, animations, 3D modeling)

2\. Soundproofing and Acoustic Design

• **Ensure:**

> o Sound-controlled rooms with acoustic panels and insulation
>
> o Noise-free environment for clear audio recordings
>
> o Isolated voice-over booths for narration and dubbing

3\. Professional Recording Equipment

• **Equip with:**

> o HD/4K video cameras and webcams
>
> o Lavalier and condenser microphones
>
> o Audio mixers and sound processors
>
> o Lighting systems (e.g., key, fill, and backlights)

4\. Editing and Post-Production Tools

• **Provide:**

> o Workstations with video/audio editing software (e.g., Adobe Premiere Pro, Audacity)
>
> o Green screens and virtual background setups
>
> o Storage solutions for large media files (e.g., NAS or cloud backup)

5\. Integration with LMS and Platforms

• **Enable:**

> o Direct integration with Learning Management Systems (LMS)
>
> o Live streaming capabilities (Zoom, MS Teams, YouTube Live)
>
> o Content export in various formats for multiplatform use

**35.27. Cafeteria / Dining Room / Mess Facility – Basic Requirements**

1\. Hygienic Cooking Infrastructure

• **Equip the facility with:**

> o Modern cooking appliances (e.g., ovens, steamers, induction units, commercial stoves)
>
> o Food-grade stainless steel equipment for preparation and storage
>
> o Proper ventilation systems, exhaust hoods, and fire safety measures

2\. Clean and Spacious Dining Area

• **Ensure:**

> o Sufficient seating capacity to accommodate peak traffic
>
> o Separate dining zones for students, faculty, and staff if needed
>
> o Ergonomically designed furniture (tables, benches, chairs)

3\. Hygiene and Sanitation Standards

• **Maintain:**

> o Daily cleaning protocols for kitchen and dining areas
>
> o Proper waste disposal system and pest control practices
>
> o Hand-washing stations, sanitizers, and clean water access

4\. Nutrition and Menu Planning

• **Provide:**

> o Balanced meal options (veg, non-veg, dietary restrictions)
>
> o Rotational menu system to ensure variety and nutrition
>
> o Trained dietician or meal planner for health-conscious offerings

5\. Regulatory Compliance and Certification

• **Adhere to:**

> o FSSAI guidelines and food safety certifications
>
> o Periodic health inspections and staff hygiene checks
>
> o Display of license and quality assurance certificates

**35.28. Games & Sports Facility – Basic Requirements**

1\. Outdoor Playground

• **Establish a multi-purpose outdoor field for:**

> o Football, cricket, hockey, and athletics
>
> o Track & field zones with standard measurements

• Include floodlights for evening activities and drainage systems for weather resistance

2\. Indoor Stadium

• **Construct an indoor sports complex to support:**

> o Badminton, table tennis, basketball, volleyball, chess, and carrom
>
> o Yoga and fitness sessions

• Provide wooden or synthetic flooring and safety padding

3\. Equipment and Resources

• **Maintain:**

> o Sports equipment inventory for all games
>
> o Storage units for secure and organized access
>
> o Scoreboards, nets, cones, and practice gear

4\. Training and Support Facilities

• **Appoint:**

> o Qualified coaches and physical trainers
>
> o Medical/first-aid support near sports areas
>
> o Changing rooms, restrooms, and water points

5\. Accessibility and Inclusiveness

• **Ensure:**

> o Facilities are accessible to all, including PwD students
>
> o Reserved slots or adaptations for inclusive participation
>
> o Sports activities integrated into academic calendar

**35.29. Games & Sports Facility – Aspirational Requirements**

1\. Gymnasium and Workout Center

• **Fully-equipped fitness center with:**

> o Cardio machines (treadmills, cross trainers, bikes)
>
> o Strength training equipment (free weights, multi-gyms, resistance machines)
>
> o Space for functional training, stretching, and yoga

2\. Swimming Pool

• Standard-size pool (25m or Olympic length) with:

> o Filtration and water treatment systems
>
> o Locker rooms and showers
>
> o Trained lifeguards and safety protocols

3\. High-Tech Playgrounds

• **Outdoor fields with:**

> o Synthetic turf or modern surfaces
>
> o Smart lighting, CCTV, and digital scoreboard systems
>
> o Automated irrigation and maintenance tools

4\. Modern Indoor Stadium

• **Multi-purpose arena supporting:**

> o Basketball, volleyball, badminton, futsal, and martial arts
>
> o Retractable seating and acoustic insulation
>
> o Climate control and energy-efficient lighting
>
> o Digital booking and event management system

5\. Advanced Sports Analytics & Technology

• **Incorporate:**

> o Wearable tech and performance tracking tools
>
> o AI-based coaching aids and motion analysis systems
>
> o Interactive walls and VR-based training modules

**35.30. Auditorium and Conference Rooms – Basic Requirements**

1\. Auditorium Facility

• **Construct one main auditorium with:**

> o Seating capacity suitable for full institutional gatherings (e.g., convocation, seminars, cultural events)
>
> o Raised stage, podium, and green rooms for presenters/performers
>
> o Good acoustics and soundproofing

2\. Audio-Visual and Lighting Systems

• **Equip auditorium with:**

> o PA system, wireless microphones, speakers, and amplifiers
>
> o Projector and LED screen or large digital display
>
> o Stage lighting, dimmers, and ambient lighting for mood control

3\. Conference Rooms (Multiple Capacities)

• **Provide a range of conference rooms with:**

> o Seating capacities (10–20 for meetings, 30–100 for workshops/seminars)
>
> o Ergonomic furniture and modular seating
>
> o Whiteboards, projectors, and interactive displays

4\. Connectivity and Digital Access

• **Ensure:**

> o Wi-Fi and LAN connectivity in all rooms
>
> o Video conferencing equipment for hybrid sessions
>
> o Recording and streaming support for digital archiving

5\. Environmental Comfort

• **Include:**

> o Air conditioning, ventilation, and emergency exits
>
> o Accessible design (PwD-friendly entrances and seating)
>
> o Fire safety systems, CCTV, and backup power

**35.31. Student Hostels – Basic Requirements**

1\. Capacity and Accessibility

• Provide accommodation for at least 60% of enrolled students

• Prioritize outstation and economically disadvantaged students

2\. Room Infrastructure

• **Offer a mix of:**

> o Single, double, and triple occupancy rooms
>
> o **Basic furniture:** bed, mattress, study table, chair, cupboard
>
> o Adequate ventilation, lighting, and ceiling fans

3\. Sanitation and Hygiene

• **Ensure:**

> o Clean and well-maintained washrooms (attached or shared)
>
> o Regular housekeeping services
>
> o Clean drinking water (RO system) and sanitation facilities

4\. Mess and Dining Area

• **On-premises mess facility with:**

> o Nutritious and hygienic food (veg and non-veg options)
>
> o Separate dining area with adequate seating

5\. Safety and Security

• **Implement:**

> o 24x7 security personnel and CCTV surveillance
>
> o Biometric or ID-based access
>
> o Fire safety systems and first-aid facilities

6\. Utilities and Amenities

• **Include:**

> o Wi-Fi and power backup
>
> o Laundry area or services
>
> o Common rooms with TV, newspapers, and indoor games

**35.32. Research Scholars Hostels – Desirable Requirements**

1\. Private and Comfortable Accommodation

• Offer single occupancy rooms or studio apartments

• **Include:**

> o Bed, study table, bookshelves, ergonomic chair
>
> o Attached bathroom, kitchenette (optional), and balcony (if feasible)

2\. High-Speed Internet and Digital Access

• **Provide:**

> o Dedicated high-speed Wi-Fi or LAN connectivity
>
> o Access to digital libraries and institutional intranet

3\. Study and Research Environment

• **Include:**

> o Dedicated reading/study lounges
>
> o Mini discussion/conference rooms for collaborative work
>
> o Soundproof zones for quiet study

4\. Wellness and Lifestyle Facilities

• **Provide:**

> o Recreational rooms, indoor games, and fitness zones
>
> o Cafeteria or pantry space within hostel premises
>
> o Laundry machines and drying area

5\. Safety and Autonomy

• **Ensure:**

> o Secure and private access with biometric/ID card entry
>
> o 24/7 surveillance and wardens familiar with research schedules
>
> o Late-night access flexibility for research needs

6\. Support Services

• **Offer:**

> o Counselling services, mentoring, and wellness support
>
> o Housekeeping and maintenance assistance
>
> o Transport support to library/labs during odd hours

**35.33. Parking – Basic Requirements**

1\. Inclusive Design and Capacity

• **Provide adequate parking space for:**

> o Students
>
> o Faculty and administrative staff
>
> o Visitors and guests

• Allocate separate zones for two-wheelers, four-wheelers, and bicycles

2\. Accessibility and PwD Provisions

• **Ensure:**

> o Designated accessible parking spots for Persons with Disabilities (PwD)
>
> o Ramps and wide access lanes for easy mobility

3\. Safety and Security

• **Install:**

> o CCTV surveillance and proper lighting
>
> o Security personnel or automated gate system
>
> o Clearly marked signs and directional arrows

4\. Environmental Considerations

• **Include:**

> o Green paving (permeable surfaces)
>
> o Tree shading and designated EV charging stations
>
> o Rainwater drainage systems

5\. Operational Management

• **Maintain:**

> o Digital pass or token systems for regulation
>
> o Visitor log or automated ticketing system
>
> o Maintenance schedule for cleanliness and repairs

**35.34. International Student Hostels – Aspirational Requirements**

1\. Premium Accommodation Standards

• **Provide well-furnished private or shared rooms with:**

> o Attached bathrooms, mini-kitchenettes, and climate control (AC/heating)
>
> o Ergonomic furniture, personal wardrobes, and study desks
>
> o Soundproofing and modern interiors reflecting international comfort levels

2\. Multicultural Living Environment

• **Design communal spaces to encourage:**

> o Cross-cultural interaction through lounges, shared kitchens, and dining areas
>
> o Cultural display zones and community bulletin boards
>
> o Dedicated language and cultural orientation rooms

3\. Advanced Amenities

• **Include:**

> o High-speed internet (LAN/Wi-Fi) and IPTV services
>
> o Laundry services, pantry areas, and 24/7 hot water
>
> o Automated access systems (RFID/Biometric) for security

4\. Support and Integration Services

• **Offer:**

> o International student support office within hostel premises
>
> o Orientation programs, cultural mentorship, and buddy systems
>
> o Emergency helpline, multilingual signage, and trained staff

5\. Recreation and Wellbeing

• **Provide:**

> o Indoor recreation room, gym/yoga room, and green open spaces
>
> o Quiet zones for reading and study
>
> o Wellness and counselling access nearby

6\. Location and Transport

• **Ensure hostels are:**

> o Proximate to academic blocks or transport-connected
>
> o Equipped with shuttle services for city or campus travel

**35.35. Exhibition Hall – Basic Requirements**

1\. Purpose and Utility

• **Designed to support:**

> o Academic exhibitions (project displays, science fairs)
>
> o Vocational/Skill showcases (crafts, models, industrial demos)
>
> o Cultural and innovation events

2\. Space and Layout

• **Provide:**

> o Flexible open space with modular partitioning options
>
> o Wide entry/exit points for crowd movement
>
> o Adequate ceiling height for large exhibits or installations

3\. Infrastructure and Facilities

• **Equipped with:**

> o Display panels, podiums, and movable boards
>
> o Lighting systems (track lights, focus lights for exhibits)
>
> o Electrical outlets and AV support (projectors, screens, sound)

4\. Connectivity and Technology

• **Ensure:**

> o Wi-Fi access, digital signage, and QR code-linked exhibit systems
>
> o Live streaming/recording facilities for virtual showcases

5\. Accessibility and Compliance

• **Provide:**

> o PwD-friendly access, ramps, and elevators (if multi-floor)
>
> o Fire exits, extinguishers, and emergency lighting

6\. Supporting Amenities

• **Include:**

> o Storage rooms for materials and setups
>
> o Green rooms or prep rooms for participants
>
> o Restrooms nearby and basic refreshments corner

**35.36. Guest Accommodation – Basic Requirements**

1\. Capacity and Room Setup

• **Provide:**

> o Single and double occupancy rooms with ensuite bathrooms
>
> o Minimum 4–10 rooms to accommodate external faculty, examiners, visiting dignitaries, or collaborators

2\. Essential Furnishings and Comfort

• **Include:**

> o Bed, mattress, writing desk, chairs, and wardrobe
>
> o Air conditioning or ventilation systems
>
> o Basic kitchen or pantry area for tea/coffee preparation

3\. Hygiene and Maintenance

• **Ensure:**

> o Daily housekeeping services
>
> o Clean and sanitized bathrooms with hot water supply
>
> o Fresh linens and toiletries for each guest

4\. Safety and Security

• **Provide:**

> o 24/7 security personnel or gated access
>
> o CCTV monitoring in common areas
>
> o Fire safety systems and first-aid kit

5\. Connectivity and Communication

• **Offer:**

> o Wi-Fi and telephone/intercom services
>
> o Information board or booklet with campus map and key contact numbers

6\. Common Facilities

• **Include access to:**

> o Dining area or cafeteria
>
> o Reception/concierge desk
>
> o Common lounge or meeting space

**35.37. Guest Accommodation – Star Hotel Type (Desirable Requirements)**

1\. Premium Accommodation Facilities

• Provide luxuriously furnished suites and rooms, including:

> o King/Queen-sized beds, plush furniture, and modern interiors
>
> o Attached washrooms with premium fittings and 24/7 hot water
>
> o Climate control systems (AC/heating) and blackout curtains

2\. In-Room Amenities

• **Include:**

> o Flat-screen TV with DTH/cable
>
> o High-speed Wi-Fi, minibar, kettle, and workspace
>
> o Digital access systems (key cards or biometric entry)

3\. Dining and Cuisine

• **Offer:**

> o Multi-cuisine cafeteria or in-house dining
>
> o Room service or buffet options
>
> o Special diet menus on request

4\. Recreation and Wellness

• **Provide:**

> o Fitness centre/gym and spa
>
> o Indoor games, lounges, or reading rooms
>
> o Access to garden areas or walking trails

5\. Guest Services

• **Ensure:**

> o 24/7 front desk, concierge, and travel desk
>
> o Laundry, dry cleaning, and housekeeping
>
> o Taxi/shuttle arrangements and city/local guides

6\. Safety and Accessibility

• **Include:**

> o CCTV surveillance, electronic safes, and secure access points
>
> o Fire exits, extinguishers, emergency lighting
>
> o Wheelchair-accessible design and assistive features

**35.38. Commercial Shops/Centers – Basic Requirements**

1\. Purpose and Accessibility

• **Establish on-campus convenience shops to serve:**

> o Students, faculty, staff, and visitors
>
> o Located near hostels, academic blocks, and common areas

2\. Essential Goods and Services

• **Provide access to:**

> o **Daily essentials:** snacks, stationery, hygiene products, water, SIM recharge
>
> o **Basic health supplies:** over-the-counter medicines, masks, sanitizers
>
> o Copying/printing services for academic materials

3\. Infrastructure and Setup

• **Ensure:**

> o Well-ventilated, organized, and hygienic retail spaces
>
> o Covered walkways and signage
>
> o Basic security and lighting provisions

4\. Vendor Management and Regulation

• **Define:**

> o Fair pricing mechanisms and operating hours
>
> o Licensing and safety compliance for vendors
>
> o SOPs for cleanliness, waste disposal, and inventory checks

5\. Optional Additions

• **Encourage:**

> o Seasonal pop-up stalls for books, university merchandise, etc.
>
> o Digital payment facilities and order-on-call services

**35.39. Commercial Shops/Centers – Aspirational Requirements**

1\. Multi-Utility Shopping Complex

• Develop a dedicated commercial hub within campus premises offering:

> o Retail outlets for clothing, electronics, books, and personal care
>
> o Specialty stores like opticians, gift shops, and university merchandise

2\. Food and Beverage Options

• **Include:**

> o Branded food chains, cafés, juice bars, and bakeries
>
> o Local cuisine outlets to support cultural diversity
>
> o Seating areas for casual dining and social interaction

3\. Student-Centric Services

• **Provide:**

> o Bookstores and stationery megastores
>
> o Courier services, travel agencies, ATMs, and banking facilities
>
> o Salon/spa and laundry outlets

4\. Design and Infrastructure

• **Ensure:**

> o Modern mall-like ambience with air conditioning, signage, and elevators
>
> o Ample lighting, clean pathways, and green spaces around the complex
>
> o Security measures such as CCTV, guards, and biometric access zones

5\. Smart and Sustainable Facilities

• **Integrate:**

> o Digital kiosks for navigation and service feedback
>
> o Solar panels, waste segregation units, and rainwater harvesting
>
> o EV charging stations and bicycle racks nearby

6\. Governance and Vendor Management

• **Implement:**

> o Rental space allotment policy for local vendors and startups
>
> o Student startup incubation kiosks for selling products/services
>
> o Regular audits for hygiene, pricing, and customer satisfaction

**35.40. Health and Well-being – Modern Dispensary / Hospital (Desirable Requirements)**

1\. 24/7 Medical Services

• **Operate a fully functional healthcare facility providing:**

> o Inpatient services with adequate beds and isolation units
>
> o Outpatient consultation with general physicians and specialists
>
> o Emergency services with ambulance availability round-the-clock

2\. Healthcare Staff and Specialties

• **Include:**

> o **Qualified medical personnel:** doctors, nurses, paramedics, and mental health professionals
>
> o Visiting specialists (e.g., orthopedics, dermatology, gynecology, psychiatry)
>
> o Administrative staff for efficient patient management and records

3\. Infrastructure and Equipment

• **Provide:**

> o Pharmacy with essential medicines
>
> o Diagnostic lab for blood tests, ECG, X-ray, etc.
>
> o Minor OT (Operation Theatre) for emergency procedures
>
> o Health monitoring equipment (oxygen support, defibrillators, etc.)

4\. Mental Health and Counseling Services

• **Offer:**

> o On-site clinical psychologists or counselors
>
> o Confidential mental wellness sessions and therapy rooms
>
> o Workshops on stress management, emotional resilience, and self-care

5\. Digital Health Records and Access

• **Implement:**

> o Electronic Health Records (EHR) for all students, faculty, and staff
>
> o Appointment booking systems through web or mobile portals
>
> o Telemedicine support for remote consultation

6\. Hygiene, Accessibility, and Safety

• **Ensure:**

> o PwD-friendly access, clean surroundings, and waste management systems
>
> o Separate male and female wards
>
> o Emergency contact desk and health helpline numbers

**35.41. Student Recreation Facilities – Desirable Requirements**

1\. Multipurpose Recreation Centre

• **Establish a central recreation hub with:**

> o Indoor games area (table tennis, carrom, chess, foosball)
>
> o Lounge spaces for relaxation, reading, and peer interaction
>
> o Music and dance rooms with acoustic setups

2\. Creative and Cultural Facilities

• **Include:**

> o Art and craft studios, pottery corners, and painting areas
>
> o Auditorium or open-air theatre for cultural performances, screenings, and student events
>
> o Photography and film clubs with editing workstations

3\. Fitness and Wellness Amenities

• **Offer:**

> o Yoga and meditation rooms
>
> o Aerobics/dance fitness studios
>
> o Gaming zones with virtual/AR experiences and e-sports lounges

4\. Tech and Innovation Corners

• **Provide:**

> o Digital media zones for video editing, podcasting, and content creation
>
> o VR/AR interactive stations for immersive recreation
>
> o Hackathon-ready spaces for creative coding marathons

5\. Outdoor Facilities

• **Complement with:**

> o Landscaped gardens, gazebos, and open discussion pods
>
> o Skating rinks or cycling tracks
>
> o Amphitheatres for informal gatherings and student-led workshops

6\. Accessibility and Safety

• **Ensure:**

> o Inclusive design for differently-abled students
>
> o CCTV surveillance, first-aid kits, and secure access
>
> o Sustainable features like energy-efficient lighting and natural ventilation

**35.42. International Student Centres – Aspirational Requirements**

1\. Dedicated Facility and Lounge Areas

• **Establish a modern international student centre offering:**

> o Welcome desk and orientation zone for new arrivals
>
> o Lounge spaces for networking, socialising, and relaxation
>
> o Cultural display zones to celebrate diversity and intercultural learning

2\. Administrative and Support Services

• **Provide integrated support for:**

> o Visa assistance, FRRO registration, and legal help
>
> o Academic advising tailored to international credit systems
>
> o Language labs and translation/interpreter support

3\. Well-being and Cultural Integration

• **Offer:**

> o Peer mentoring and buddy systems for smooth adaptation
>
> o Workshops on local customs, academic etiquette, and safety norms
>
> o Regular intercultural festivals and global food fairs

4\. Amenities and Infrastructure

• **Include:**

> o Wi-Fi enabled study zones, small meeting rooms, and printing services
>
> o Prayer/meditation rooms, lockers, and hygiene facilities
>
> o 24x7 help desk and emergency support system

5\. Residential and Dining Support

• **Coordinate with:**

> o International hostels or dedicated accommodation blocks
>
> o Mess/cafeteria with international cuisine options
>
> o Access to local grocery stores and cooking areas

6\. Institutional Integration

• **Promote:**

> o Cross-cultural academic programs and workshops
>
> o Exchange programs and joint degree pathways
>
> o Inclusion in student councils and leadership bodies

**35.43. Incubation Centre and Research Park – Aspirational Requirements**

1\. State-of-the-Art Infrastructure

• **Build a dedicated innovation ecosystem that includes:**

> o Modular workspaces, co-working labs, and private R&D cabins
>
> o Meeting rooms, event halls, and seminar spaces with AV facilities
>
> o 24/7 access, security, and high-speed internet

2\. In-House Industry R&D Units

• **Host:**

> o Partner companies’ innovation wings directly on campus
>
> o Startup offices focused on tech, biotech, AI, clean energy, etc.
>
> o Prototype development zones with rapid tooling, 3D printing, and testing labs

3\. Academia-Industry Collaboration

• **Facilitate:**

> o Joint research projects between faculty, students, and industry experts
>
> o Live problem-solving labs for real-world innovation challenges
>
> o Industry-sponsored internships and thesis supervision

4\. Startup Support and Commercialization

• **Provide:**

> o Seed funding, angel network access, and IP/legal consultation
>
> o Entrepreneurship bootcamps and mentorship from domain experts
>
> o Market readiness programs for product validation and go-to-market strategy

5\. Integration with Curriculum

• **Enable:**

> o Student involvement in ongoing startup projects
>
> o Credits for entrepreneurship and innovation lab work
>
> o Hackathons, innovation challenges, and demo days

6\. Sustainable and Scalable Design

• **Implement:**

> o Green building features, energy-efficient systems, and digital control
>
> o Scalable modules to add new industry units and research wings
>
> o Data-sharing infrastructure for cross-domain collaboration

**35.44. Botanical Park / Garden – Aspirational Requirements**

1\. Scientific and Educational Purpose

• **Establish a documented collection of:**

> o Indigenous and exotic plant species
>
> o Medicinal plants, aromatic herbs, and endangered flora
>
> o Thematic plant zones (e.g., rainforest, desert, aquatic ecosystems)

2\. Infrastructure and Layout

• **Include:**

> o Labeled plant beds with botanical names and QR-coded information
>
> o Walking trails, arboretums, and interpretive signboards
>
> o Observation decks and greenhouses for sensitive species

3\. Research and Conservation Facilities

• **Support:**

> o Live specimen conservation programs for biodiversity preservation
>
> o Herbariums and seed banks for academic research
>
> o Collaborations with botany departments and environmental NGOs

4\. Educational and Community Engagement

• **Organize:**

> o Field trips, workshops, and guided tours for students
>
> o Botany awareness programs for local communities and schools
>
> o Citizen science initiatives like tree-planting and flora monitoring

5\. Eco-Friendly Design

• **Implement:**

> o Rainwater harvesting, solar-powered lighting, and composting units
>
> o Natural landscaping using native species for minimal water use
>
> o Sustainable maintenance with minimal chemical inputs

6\. Accessibility and Visitor Amenities

• **Ensure:**

> o Wheelchair-accessible paths, resting areas, and shaded seating
>
> o Educational kiosks, signboards, and mobile apps for self-guided tours
>
> o Botanical café or eco-shop featuring plant-based and local products

**35.45. Vocational Education, Training, and Skilling Infrastructure**

1\. Dedicated Building Infrastructure

• Construct purpose-built spaces for hands-on learning and skilling:

> o Separate vocational training blocks for each trade or sector
>
> o Workshops and practice labs designed as per industry norms
>
> o Flexible layouts to support modular skilling programs

2\. Equipment and Tools

• Provide industry-grade tools and machinery, such as:

> o Mechanical and electrical toolkits, CNC machines, automotive benches
>
> o Sewing machines, carpentry tools, food tech instruments, etc.
>
> o Safety gear and operational manuals for all tools

3\. Technology and Computer Labs

• **Ensure modern computer labs with:**

> o Internet-enabled systems for skill simulations and virtual training
>
> o Access to CAD/CAM software, digital design tools, and skill-focused apps
>
> o Learning Management Systems (LMS) for tracking practical skill progression

4\. Sector-Specific Skill Zones

• **Create domain-specific environments such as:**

> o Hospitality training kitchens, mock hotel rooms
>
> o Retail POS training zones, banking/finance simulation counters
>
> o Health care skill stations, patient care simulation labs

5\. Pedagogical Support Facilities

• **Include:**

> o Smart classrooms for theory integration
>
> o Demo areas, virtual reality zones, and e-learning pods
>
> o Audio-visual systems for practical demonstrations

6\. Accreditation and Industry Linkage

• **Align infrastructure with:**

> o NSQF levels, Sector Skill Councils (SSCs) standards
>
> o Industry certification programs, apprenticeship support
>
> o On-site assessment and certification stations

**36. Details of digital infrastructure**

**36.1. Internet Usage in Higher Education Institutions**

1\. Purpose and Function

• **Facilitate connection to the external world for:**

> o Students, faculty, staff, and researchers
>
> o Academic, administrative, and recreational purposes

2\. Modes of Access

• **Enable usage through:**

> o Laptops, desktops, tablets, smartphones
>
> o Campus-wide Wi-Fi networks with seamless connectivity
>
> o LAN connectivity in high-bandwidth areas like labs and admin blocks

3\. Applications and Benefits

• **Academic:**

> o Access to e-resources, MOOCs, journals, and digital libraries
>
> o Participation in online classes, webinars, and conferences
>
> o Collaborative research and knowledge sharing globally

• **Administrative:**

> o Streamlined admission, fee payment, and academic record systems
>
> o Cloud-based MIS and ERP platforms for real-time data access

• **Social and Personal:**

> o Engagement in student communities, forums, and alumni platforms
>
> o Exposure to global trends and opportunities

4\. Security and Policy

• **Ensure:**

> o Safe browsing environments with firewalls and content filters
>
> o Security protocols and user authentication systems
>
> o Clear Acceptable Use Policy (AUP) for ethical and legal compliance

5\. Monitoring and Optimization

• **Monitor:**

> o Bandwidth usage, uptime, and load balancing
>
> o User behavior analytics for academic enhancement
>
> o Use of AI-based systems to prevent misuse or overload

**36.2. Institutional Website – Core Role and Requirements**

1\. Primary Purpose

• Serve as the official digital face of the institution

• Disseminate authentic, up-to-date information to the public

2\. Key Functionalities

• **Information Gateway:**

> o **About the institution:** history, vision, mission, leadership
>
> o Academic programs, departments, and faculty profiles
>
> o Admission criteria, important dates, and application portals

• **Communication Hub:**

> o News updates, circulars, and event announcements
>
> o Contact information, grievance redressal forms, and feedback options

• **Service Integration:**

> o Access to student portals, ERP systems, e-payment gateways
>
> o Online application forms, result publication, and alumni registration

3\. Technical and Design Standards

• **Ensure:**

> o Responsive design for mobile and desktop compatibility
>
> o Multi-language support (especially English and regional languages)
>
> o Fast loading speed, minimal downtime, and search engine optimization (SEO)

4\. Accessibility and Compliance

• **Comply with:**

> o Government website guidelines (GIGW) and accessibility standards (WCAG 2.1)
>
> o UGC/AICTE-mandated disclosures and regulatory transparency

5\. Content Management and Security

• Use a secure CMS (Content Management System) with:

> o Role-based access for content editors
>
> o SSL certificates and regular backup schedules
>
> o Data protection measures and real-time monitoring

**36.3. Online Messaging for Stakeholder Groups**

*1. Purpose*

• Facilitate vertical communication (top-down and bottom-up)

• Support horizontal communication (peer-to-peer, interdepartmental)

• Enable real-time collaboration among all stakeholder groups

*2. Target Stakeholder Groups*

• Students (UG, PG, PhD)

• Faculty and academic staff

• Administrative and support staff

• Alumni and external partners

• Parents and guardians (where applicable)

*3. Communication Channels*

• Institutional messaging platforms (e.g., Microsoft Teams, Slack, Mattermost)

• Intranet-based bulletin boards and announcement portals

• Email groups and SMS notifications for formal updates

• Push notifications via mobile apps or learning management systems (LMS)

*4. Key Features*

• Group messaging, tagging, and threaded discussions

• Broadcast alerts (e.g., exam schedules, closures, emergencies)

• Integration with event calendars, task tracking, and feedback forms

• Support for file sharing, video calls, and meeting scheduling

*5. Governance and Etiquette*

• Maintain a communication policy to define use-cases and tone

• Assign moderators/admins to manage channels and users

• Promote inclusive, respectful communication practices

*6. Benefits*

• Enhances transparency and accountability

• Improves stakeholder engagement and sense of community

• Accelerates decision-making and reduces email overload

**36.4. Online Blogs & Course-Specific Sites**

1\. Purpose

• Offer transparent access to course content and updates

• Document day-to-day learning progress for enrolled students

• Engage stakeholders (students, parents, faculty, administrators, employers) and publics

2\. Key Features

• **Course overview:** syllabus, objectives, outcomes

• **Daily/weekly updates:** lecture summaries, reading materials, activities

• **Assessment logs:** quizzes, assignments, project milestones, feedback

• **Student engagement:** discussion boards, comments, polls, Q&A

• **Resources section:** links to external readings, videos, tools

3\. Technical Implementation

• **Use platforms like:**

> o WordPress, Google Sites, GitHub Pages for standalone blogs
>
> o **LMS integration:** Moodle, Canvas, Blackboard with blog plug-ins
>
> o Institutional portals with role-based access for each course

4\. Stakeholder Benefits

• **Students:** stay on track, revisit materials, and showcase their work

• **Faculty:** monitor student progress and collect feedback

• **Parents:** track academic engagement and curriculum relevance

• **Employers:** understand curriculum and student competencies

5\. Compliance & Content Standards

• **Adhere to:**

> o Privacy norms (GDPR, institutional policies) for student data
>
> o Academic integrity and copyright standards for materials
>
> o Regular content moderation and accessibility standards

6\. Long-Term Advantages

• Build digital learning portfolios for students

• Archive course history for continuous improvement

• Strengthen public trust through curriculum transparency

**36.5. Wi-Fi Campus**

1\. Purpose

• Enable ubiquitous access to online educational resources

• Support seamless digital learning inside classrooms, labs, libraries, and hostels

• Facilitate research, collaboration, and communication through uninterrupted connectivity

2\. Key Features

• **Campus-wide coverage:** including academic blocks, admin areas, hostels, cafeterias, and open spaces

• **High-speed internet:** with scalable bandwidth based on user demand

• **Secure authentication system:** using student/staff credentials (Eduroam, captive portals)

3\. Infrastructure Requirements

• Access Points (APs): strategically installed to eliminate dead zones

• Central Network Operations Center (NOC): for real-time monitoring and management

• **Load balancing and redundancy:** to ensure consistent performance during peak hours

4\. User Management

• **Role-based access:**

> o Students, faculty, researchers, staff, and guests

• Time-based usage limits (if needed) for non-academic activities

• Compliance with Acceptable Use Policy (AUP) and data logging for security

5\. Benefits

• Promotes blended and flipped classrooms

• Enables on-demand access to LMS, digital libraries, and e-content

• Supports innovation, e-governance, and smart campus integration

6\. Maintenance and Upgrades

• Conduct periodic audits for signal strength and device compatibility

• Regular firmware and security updates

• Integrate with firewalls and antivirus systems to prevent cyber threats

**36.6. Online Study Material**

1\. Purpose

• Provide additional academic support alongside classroom teaching

• Ensure ubiquitous access to learning materials for all students

• Enable multi-format learning to suit diverse learner needs and preferences

2\. Content Formats

• **Text:** PDFs, eBooks, lecture notes, summaries

• **Audio:** Recorded lectures, podcasts, spoken word modules

• **Video:** Lecture recordings, tutorials, animated explainers, lab demos

3\. Delivery Platforms

• Learning Management Systems (LMS): Moodle, Google Classroom, Canvas

• **Institutional Portals:** with secure login and download options

• **Mobile-Compatible Apps:** for use on smartphones and tablets

• **Cloud Storage Links:** Google Drive, OneDrive, etc., with controlled access

4\. Storage & Accessibility

• **All materials should be:**

> o Downloadable in formats like PDF, MP4, MP3
>
> o Compatible with smartphones, tablets, and laptops
>
> o Offline-accessible for learners with limited internet access

5\. Academic Integration

• Align material directly with syllabus and lesson plans

• **Update regularly with:**

> o New editions, current examples, recent discoveries

• **Include self-assessment tools:** quizzes, reflections, activity checklists

6\. Benefits

• Supports repetition and revision at students’ convenience

• Encourages self-paced learning

• Increases student engagement and retention of concepts

• Facilitates inclusive education for students with varied learning needs

**36.7. Digital Library**

1\. Purpose

• Provide ubiquitous access to academic resources for all stakeholders

• Support anytime-anywhere learning through a centralized digital knowledge hub

• Promote research, continuous learning, and academic excellence

2\. Membership and Access

• **Mandatory digital library accounts for:**

> o Students, faculty, researchers, administrative staff

• Role-based access levels to content depending on user type

• Single Sign-On (SSO) or institutional credentials for secure entry

3\. Content Types

• Books and eBooks (subject-specific, general knowledge)

• Academic Journals (national and international)

• Periodicals and magazines

• Theses and dissertations

• Government publications, annual/yearbooks

• Study materials and lecture content

• Multimedia content (video lectures, webinars, archives)

4\. Platform Integration

• **Integration with:**

> o National Digital Library of India (NDLI)
>
> o Shodhganga, INFLIBNET, DELNET, e-ShodhSindhu
>
> o Global libraries (e.g., World Digital Library, JSTOR, IEEE Xplore)

• Utilize Open Access Repositories and institutional servers

5\. Functional Features

• Searchable database with advanced filters

• Download and offline access options

• Usage tracking and analytics for performance and planning

• Bookmarking, citation export, and annotation tools

6\. Benefits

• Promotes digital inclusion and reduces dependency on physical libraries

• Increases reading habits and research output

• Supports remote learning and enhances the university's academic reputation

• Reduces cost and improves efficiency in resource dissemination

**36.8. Digital Publication**

1\. Purpose

• Enable the university to create, publish, and distribute its academic and institutional content

• Promote research visibility and academic communication

• Support open-access dissemination for global academic contribution

2\. Types of Publications

• **Books and Monographs:** authored by faculty, researchers, or students

• Newsletters & Magazines: periodic updates on institutional activities, events, achievements

• Research Journals & Proceedings: peer-reviewed journals, conference papers

• **Question Papers:** secure and digital distribution for exams

3\. Platform and Tools

• University digital publication portal (with DOI assignment and archive management)

• **Online Publishing Software:**

> o Open Journal Systems (OJS)
>
> o Adobe InDesign / Scribus for layout

• Digital Object Identifier (DOI) for scholarly articles and books

4\. Open Access Model

• Host publications on an open-access platform

• Collaborate with National Digital Library, UGC-CARE, DOAJ, etc.

• Ensure metadata standards (e.g., Dublin Core) for discoverability

5\. Process and Governance

• Establish a Publication Committee for editorial, peer review, and copyright handling

• Implement plagiarism checks and content quality assurance

• Digitally archive all publications in the university’s institutional repository

6\. Benefits

• Enhances the university’s academic profile and research output

• Increases global visibility and citation potential

• Ensures transparency and timely dissemination of institutional materials

• Reduces printing costs and supports sustainability

**36.9. Paperless Office**

1\. Purpose

• Eliminate paper-based workflows by creating a fully digital academic and administrative ecosystem

• Increase efficiency, transparency, and accountability in stakeholder services

• Reduce operational costs and environmental impact

2\. Key Functional Areas

• **Student Services:** admissions, fee payments, transcripts, certificates, ID cards

• **Faculty Services:** leave applications, attendance, course assignments, salary slips

• **Administrative Services:** HR records, procurement, scheduling, approvals

• **Examination Services:** digital question paper generation, marks entry, results publication

3\. Core Components

• Academic Administrative Software (ERP/EMS system)

• Document Management System (DMS)

• Workflow Automation (e-forms, digital approvals, task trackers)

• Role-Based Dashboards for students, faculty, and staff

4\. Tools and Technologies

• Cloud-based solutions (e.g., Google Workspace, Microsoft 365, Zoho)

• University Management System (UMS) or Student Information System (SIS)

• Secure e-signature integration (e.g., DocuSign, Adobe Sign)

• Mobile App Access for quick interaction

5\. Implementation Strategy

• Digitize existing records (scanning, metadata tagging)

• Train staff and faculty in digital tools and compliance practices

• Establish IT support team for continuous monitoring and maintenance

• Pilot phase rollout, followed by phased institution-wide implementation

6\. Benefits

• Speeds up decision-making through real-time digital access

• Enhances data security, version control, and audit trails

• Improves service delivery and user satisfaction

• Promotes a green and sustainable campus

**36.10. Paperless Exams**

1\. Purpose

• Replace traditional pen-and-paper exams with a digital examination system

• Promote eco-friendly practices and reduce paper wastage

• Improve efficiency, security, and transparency in the assessment process

2\. Components of a Digital Examination System

• **Online Exam Platforms:**

> o MCQ/Subjective formats with auto-grading support
>
> o Secure login and proctoring features

• **Question Bank Management:**

> o Encrypted question generation and storage
>
> o Randomization of questions and options

• **Digital Answer Scripts:**

> o Typed or digitally handwritten submissions
>
> o Upload mechanism for diagrams (if applicable)

3\. Required Infrastructure

• Computer labs/tablet-enabled classrooms with stable internet

• Secure exam browser software to prevent cheating

• Cloud-based server or LMS integration for hosting exams

• Digital evaluation tools for examiners (annotation, scoring interface)

4\. Implementation Steps

• Design a policy framework for online exams and invigilation

• Train faculty and students on using the platform

• Run mock tests to check readiness and technical reliability

• Ensure data backup, audit logs, and result analytics

5\. Benefits

• Saves time and reduces logistics costs

• Allows real-time result processing and analysis

• Enhances exam flexibility and accessibility (remote participation)

• Strengthens exam security and integrity through digital tools

**36.11. Online Evaluation**

1\. Purpose

• Transition from manual checking to a fully digitized evaluation system

• Eliminate paper-based answer scripts and speed up the grading process

• Ensure accuracy, transparency, and efficiency in assessments

2\. Core Features

• **Digital Answer Script Access:**

> o Students upload responses or answer within a digital platform
>
> o Evaluators access scanned or typed scripts online

• **Auto-Grading System:**

> o Supports objective/multiple-choice questions
>
> o Instant scoring and feedback generation

• **Manual Digital Evaluation:**

> o Annotate, highlight, and assign marks using digital tools
>
> o Role-based access for moderators and quality checkers

3\. Infrastructure and Platforms

• Learning Management Systems (LMS) with evaluation modules

• AI-powered auto-evaluation tools for initial assessment

• Cloud storage for script archival and security

4\. Implementation Strategy

• Set up standard digital evaluation rubrics

• Train evaluators on software tools and protocols

• Conduct dry runs and mock evaluations before live deployment

• Integrate with student information systems for result publishing

5\. Advantages

• Reduces evaluator workload and manual error

• Enables faster turnaround of exam results

• Facilitates analytics and performance reports for continuous improvement

• Improves data security and long-term digital archiving

• Enhance credibility and convenience in academic records management

• Support the Government of India's Digital India initiative

2\. Core Features

• **Digital Academic Records:**

> o Digitally signed mark sheets, certificates, and transcripts
>
> o Accessible any-time by students and authorized institutions

• **Verification Mechanism:**

> o Instant verification by employers, other universities, and government agencies
>
> o Eliminates the need for physical document submission

• **Secure Storage:**

> o Tamper-proof, encrypted storage on a national-level platform (e.g., DigiLocker)

3\. Institutional Integration

• **Mandatory Upload by HEIs:**

> o Institutions upload markscards to NAD/DigiLocker via API or portal

• **Student Linking:**

> o Students receive access via Aadhaar-based authentication or registered mobile/email

4\. Implementation Steps
