• Sign MoU with DigiLocker/NAD partners (e.g., CDSL or NSDL)

• Digitize legacy academic records and maintain continuous upload

• Train admin staff on portal use and student onboarding

• Provide student awareness sessions on benefits and usage

5\. Benefits

• Fully digital, eco-friendly recordkeeping

• Streamlines academic verification for jobs, admissions, and government services

• Prevents fraud through digitally signed documents

• Convenient lifelong access to academic credentials

**36.14. Online Admission Test**

**Objective:** Facilitate seamless, accessible, and secure admission testing for candidates worldwide.

1\. Purpose and Vision

• Enable global access to university admission processes

• Ensure equity, transparency, and scalability in candidate evaluation

• Reduce logistical and geographical barriers

2\. Key Features

• **Web-based Testing Platform:**

> o Compatible across devices (desktop, tablet, smartphone)
>
> o No physical presence required; enables remote proctoring

• Adaptive & Customizable Tests:

> o Tailored for different programs (UG/PG/research)
>
> o Multilingual support for diverse applicants

• Security & Integrity Tools:

> o AI-based remote proctoring (camera, mic, screen monitoring)
>
> o Randomized question banks with time-stamping and auto-submission
>
> o Browser lockdown and face authentication options

3\. Technical Infrastructure

• Cloud-hosted assessment engine for scalability

• Data encryption for secure transmission and storage

• Integrated analytics dashboard for result tracking and cut-off settings

4\. Implementation Strategy

• Collaborate with EdTech providers or in-house IT teams

• Create a pilot trial before full-scale rollout

• Establish candidate helpdesk for technical and academic queries

• Develop contingency plans for connectivity or hardware failures

5\. Benefits

• Promotes inclusive admissions across geographies

• Reduces cost and time for both institutions and applicants

• Strengthens the digital-first institutional image

• Enables data-driven evaluation and decision-making

**36.15. Education ERP**

**Goal:** To integrate various departments for seamless, timely, and secure information exchange within the institution.

1\. Purpose and Vision

• Centralize academic and administrative operations

• Facilitate real-time access to accurate data across departments

• Enhance efficiency, transparency, and decision-making

2\. Key Functional Modules

• Student Information System (SIS): Admissions, registration, attendance, grades, transcripts

• **Human Resource Management:** Faculty/staff records, payroll, leave, appraisal

• Finance & Accounts: Budgeting, fee collection, salary disbursement

• **Academic Management:** Timetabling, course scheduling, exam management

• **Library Management:** Cataloguing, issue/return, digital library access

• Hostel & Facilities Management: Room allocation, maintenance tracking

• Inventory & Procurement: Asset tracking, purchase order workflow

• Alumni & Placement Portals: Industry relations, job posting, alumni engagement

3\. Integration Capabilities

• Unified Dashboard for administrative visibility

• Role-based access control for students, faculty, and administrators

• Integration with external systems like DigiLocker, NAD, and LMS platforms

• Mobile app and cloud access for users anytime, anywhere

4\. Implementation Strategy

• Select an Education ERP vendor or develop in-house (e.g., open-source like Fedena, or enterprise-grade like SAP, TCS iON)

• Conduct stakeholder requirement gathering and workflow mapping

• Migrate legacy data and provide user training sessions

• Define SOPs for module usage and ongoing maintenance

5\. Benefits

• Eliminates redundancy and manual processing

• Improves coordination across all departments

• Enables data-driven planning and performance tracking

• Supports paperless and sustainable administration

**36.16. Plagiarism Software Facility**

**Goal:** Ensure academic integrity and originality by providing all stakeholders with access to plagiarism detection tools.

1\. Purpose and Vision

• Uphold research ethics and maintain academic credibility

• Empower students, researchers, and faculty to produce original work

• Prevent academic misconduct and promote proper citations

2\. Key Features

• **Institution-Wide Access:**

> o Available to students, faculty, researchers, and administrative staff
>
> o Integrated with LMS and digital repositories

• **Comprehensive Document Scanning:**

> o Checks assignments, theses, research papers, articles, proposals
>
> o **Compares against a wide database:** internet sources, publications, institutional archives

• **Detailed Similarity Reports:**

> o Percentage match and source breakdown
>
> o Highlights exact phrases and sentences with citation suggestions

• **Multi-Format Support:**

> o Accepts .docx, .pdf, .ppt, .tex, etc.

3\. Implementation Strategy

• Subscribe to or deploy trusted tools like Turnitin, Urkund (Ouriginal), Grammarly Business, or open-source alternatives

• Provide individual logins or centralized submission portals

• Train faculty and students on interpretation of similarity scores

• Integrate plagiarism checks into assessment workflows (pre-submission or evaluation phase)

4\. Benefits

• Encourages academic honesty and ethical research practices

• Supports quality assurance in institutional research outputs

• Assists faculty in evaluating assignments more efficiently

• Reduces risk of reputational damage and publication retractions

**36.17. Online Digital Magazine & Student Publication**

**Goal:** Encourage student creativity and institutional visibility through regular online publishing in digital format.

1\. Purpose and Vision

• Promote student expression, creativity, and research

• Serve as a platform for showcasing achievements, projects, and opinions

• Enhance the university's digital identity and communication culture

2\. Key Features

• **Student-Centric Content:**

> o Articles, blogs, interviews, poems, artwork, photography, project summaries
>
> o Thematic issues based on festivals, social causes, innovation, etc.

• Faculty & Editorial Oversight:

> o Mentored editorial board including faculty and student representatives
>
> o Ensures quality control and ethical publishing standards

• Digital Format & Access:

> o Published regularly via university website or dedicated portal
>
> o Compatible with smartphones, tablets, and laptops
>
> o PDF, ePub, and interactive HTML versions

• **Integration with Other Platforms:**

> o Promoted through institutional LMS, social media, and newsletters
>
> o Archived in the University Digital Library or repository

3\. Implementation Strategy

• Establish a Student Publication Committee

• Design a publication calendar and submission guidelines

• Use platforms like WordPress, Medium, Canva, or Adobe InDesign for digital magazine design

• Enable peer-review or faculty feedback mechanism for academic submissions

4\. Benefits

• Builds a culture of student engagement and ownership

• Provides recognition to contributors and enhances their portfolios

• Acts as a communication bridge between administration, students, alumni, and external stakeholders

• Adds value to university branding and outreach

**36.18. Online Placement (Project, Internship, & Final)**

**Goal:** Provide seamless, technology-driven support for students' career development through an integrated, online placement ecosystem.

1\. Purpose and Vision

• Ensure ubiquitous access to placement opportunities for all students

• Create a centralized digital platform for managing internships, live projects, and job placements

• Bridge the gap between academia and industry through real-time collaboration

2\. Key Features

• **Placement Portal Integration:**

> o Online portal or app accessible 24/7 for students, faculty, and recruiters
>
> o Real-time updates on job openings, internship opportunities, deadlines, and selection stages

• Resume & Profile Management:

> o Students can upload resumes, portfolios, and project work
>
> o AI-driven resume shortlisting and recommendation features

• Online Application & Scheduling:

> o Apply directly to posted opportunities
>
> o Auto-scheduling for interviews, tests, and webinars

• **Employer Dashboard:**

> o Companies can post positions, shortlist candidates, schedule interviews
>
> o Track candidate progress and offer status

• **Virtual Career Events:**

> o Online career fairs, pre-placement talks, webinars, and mock interviews
>
> o Integration with platforms like Zoom, MS Teams, or Google Meet

3\. Implementation Strategy

• Develop or license a Placement Management System (PMS) like Superset, Internshala for Campus, or custom ERP module

• Link placement activities with academic records and skill profiles

• Assign dedicated faculty coordinators and student liaisons

• Establish industry tie-ups and MoUs to ensure a steady flow of opportunities

4\. Benefits

• Enhances career readiness and access for all students, especially those in remote locations

• Promotes transparency and efficiency in placement coordination

• Enables real-time analytics and feedback on student performance

• Increases institutional reputation by showcasing placement metrics and success stories

**36.19. Video Documentation of Each Course & Each College**

**Goal:** Promote global transparency, open access, and academic visibility through high-quality video content documenting courses and institutional highlights.

1\. Purpose and Vision

• Enhance openness and global accessibility of institutional academic offerings

• Act as a reference and promotional tool for prospective students, partners, and stakeholders

• Strengthen the HEI's digital presence and credibility

2\. Key Features

• **Course-Level Video Documentation:**

> o Video overviews of each course syllabus, objectives, outcomes, and teaching methods
>
> o Highlight faculty members, labs, and notable student work
>
> o Include demonstrations, sample lectures, and assessments explanation

• **College-Level Documentation:**

> o Showcase facilities, infrastructure, achievements, research centers, and student life
>
> o Introduce heads of departments and student support systems
>
> o Include success stories, alumni testimonials, and partnerships

• **Content Format:**

> o Short intro videos (2–3 minutes) + Full course walkthroughs (10–15 minutes)
>
> o Subtitles, multiple language support (where applicable)
>
> o Optimized for mobile and web playback (YouTube, Vimeo, university portal)

• **Platform Integration:**

> o Upload to university website, YouTube channel, MOOCs, and national repositories
>
> o Embed within Learning Management Systems (LMS)

3\. Implementation Strategy

• Form a media and content production team including videographers, editors, and scriptwriters

• Collaborate with faculty coordinators and ICT units

• Set a semester-wise calendar for video creation and updates

• Use affordable tools like OBS Studio, Camtasia, or institutional multimedia studios

4\. Benefits

• Increases transparency and trust for stakeholders globally

• Enhances student understanding and retention via visual learning

• Aids in accreditation and quality assurance with clear evidence of course delivery

• Supports internationalization efforts and global outreach

**36.22. Integration of ICCT Technologies for Service Automation**

**Goal:** Leverage cutting-edge technologies to optimize academic and administrative operations in HEIs.

1\. Key Technologies and Their Roles

• AI (Artificial Intelligence):

> o Automate administrative tasks (e.g., admissions, scheduling, chatbot queries)
>
> o Power adaptive learning systems and personalized tutoring
>
> o Predict student performance and recommend interventions

• BA (Business Analytics):

> o Analyze institutional data for decision-making (enrollment trends, retention rates)
>
> o Monitor faculty productivity, course success rates, and resource allocation

• CC (Cloud Computing):

> o Provide scalable infrastructure for ERP, LMS, and storage
>
> o Enable remote access to data, digital libraries, and online exams
>
> o Facilitate disaster recovery and data redundancy

• DS (Data Science):

> o Extract insights from student, research, and financial data
>
> o Improve curriculum design based on data-driven feedback
>
> o Support institutional ranking strategies through data analysis

• MB (Mobile-Based Technologies):

> o Deliver learning and administrative services via mobile apps
>
> o Support m-learning, attendance tracking, fee payment, and notifications

• OC (On-Demand Computing):

> o Access computing resources dynamically as per institutional needs
>
> o Reduce infrastructure costs with elastic provisioning for peak loads

• VR (Virtual Reality) & AR (Augmented Reality):

> o Create immersive learning environments for practical and lab-based education
>
> o Simulate real-world scenarios for engineering, medical, and vocational training
>
> o Enhance engagement through interactive course modules

2\. Applications in Higher Education

• Smart classrooms and digital campuses

• AI-driven examination and evaluation systems

• Cloud-based student information systems

• AR/VR-enabled e-labs and virtual field visits

• Real-time mobile dashboards for decision-makers

3\. Benefits

• Increases efficiency, accuracy, and scalability of services

• Promotes student-centric and data-driven decision-making

• Reduces operational costs and manual workload

• Enhances accessibility, engagement, and learning outcomes

**36.23. Studio for Video Online Classes**

**36.24. Video Conference Facility**

**Purpose:** Enable seamless global communication, academic exchange, and collaboration through digital platforms.

1\. Key Objectives

• Facilitate real-time interaction with national and international academic partners

• Support online meetings, guest lectures, panel discussions, and virtual classrooms

• Enable remote participation in seminars, conferences, and workshops

2\. Infrastructure Requirements

• High-speed internet with redundancy for uninterrupted connectivity

• HD video cameras, microphones, speakers, and large display screens

• Integrated software platforms (Zoom, Microsoft Teams, Google Meet, WebEx)

• Conference control system with scheduling, muting, and screen-sharing options

• Dedicated rooms with soundproofing and ergonomic furniture

3\. Functional Use-Cases

• Faculty collaboration with researchers across the globe

• Student participation in virtual exchange programs and internships

• Administrative meetings with regulatory bodies or partner institutions

• Virtual viva-voce, thesis defenses, and cross-border evaluations

4\. Benefits

• Reduces travel costs and carbon footprint

• Promotes global networking and knowledge sharing

• Enhances institutional reputation through international presence

• Enables inclusive participation irrespective of geographical constraints

**36.25. Online Open Publication System**

**Purpose:** Democratize access to newly generated academic knowledge by enabling open-access dissemination.

1\. Core Objectives

• Facilitate free and open access to research outputs, academic articles, reports, and data

• Promote academic transparency and global collaboration

• Encourage knowledge dissemination across disciplines and communities

2\. Key Features

• Institutional digital repository for storing publications, theses, dissertations, and research reports

• Integration with global open-access platforms (e.g., DOAJ, arXiv, SSRN, PubMed Central)

• Self-submission portals for faculty, researchers, and students to upload work

• Peer-review system for quality assurance and scholarly validation

• Metadata tagging for easy indexing, discoverability, and citation tracking

3\. Technical Infrastructure

• Built on open-source platforms (e.g., DSpace, Open Journal Systems)

• Digital Object Identifiers (DOI) for permanent accessibility

• Creative Commons licensing for proper attribution and usage rights

• Searchable database with filters by author, department, keywords, and year

4\. Academic Impact

• Increases visibility and citation of research outputs

• Supports institutional ranking and knowledge leadership

• Encourages a culture of publishing, especially among young researchers

• Ensures public accountability for publicly funded research

• Administrative Efficiency

> o Upgrade administrative buildings with smart governance tools and centralized systems.
>
> o Focus on digital operations for transparency and resource efficiency.

• Collaborative and Community Spaces

> o Renovate and develop lounges, seminar halls, and cafeterias for informal interactions.
>
> o Encourage interdisciplinary engagement among students, faculty, and researchers.

• Inclusive Infrastructure

> o Ensure campus facilities are accessible and safe for students and staff with disabilities.
>
> o Implement universal design principles to foster inclusivity.

• Sustainability Initiatives

> o Promote water harvesting, energy conservation, and recycling.
>
> o Expand green belts and implement eco-conscious practices.

• Zero Emission & Green Energy Strategy

> o Install centralized air conditioning and solar energy systems across buildings.
>
> o Permit and promote electric, hybrid, or clean-fuel vehicles on campus.
>
> o Use electric/clean vehicles for official University transportation.

• Cultural & International Collaboration

> o Create a dedicated centre for cultural exchange and international student orientation.
>
> o Host cross-cultural festivals and U21 collaboration trips.
>
> o Expand summer/winter schools and launch an international convention centre.

• Incubation and Research Parks

> o Develop physical infrastructure for incubators and research parks.
>
> o Provide lab space, internet, office support, R&D, mentoring, and funding assistance.

• Smart, Eco-Friendly Cafeteria

> o Build solar-powered, induction-based cafeteria with energy-efficient appliances.
>
> o Implement smart waste bins, water-saving techniques, and eco-friendly materials.
>
> o Introduce health-focused initiatives and a Working Lunch Scheme for underprivileged students.
>
> o Partner with self-help and social groups to manage procurement and surplus food distribution.

• Advanced Technological Hubs

> o Establish a State-of-the-Art Supercomputer and Media Centre.
>
> o Connect with national and global media houses and foster AI-driven research capabilities.

**36.39. Role of Physical Enabler – Mid-term Goals**

• Strengthening Sustainable Campus Practices

> o Enhance eco-friendly buildings, energy-efficient systems, and green practices.
>
> o Achieve zero-emission targets through inter-departmental coordination for green energy and self-sufficient energy production.

• Green and Sustainable Campus Development

> o Expand renewable energy use, sustainable transport, water harvesting, waste management, green landscaping, and biodiversity conservation.
>
> o Upgrade the sewage treatment plant (STP) and develop wastewater reuse systems to support a zero-waste policy.

• Modernizing Examination Infrastructure

> o Digitize historical records and implement automated grading, scheduling, and transparent revaluation systems.
>
> o Introduce inclusive, modular exam policies aligned with NEP 2020 and reduce paper use through digitization.
>
> o Launch a 24x7 smart student care center with complaint resolution and feedback systems.

• Infrastructure Expansion

> o Construct new teaching and administrative blocks.
>
> o Establish East and West Delhi campuses.
>
> o Build a world-class library system and an Innovation Library Lab focusing on emerging technologies.

• Smart Campus Implementation

> o Complete deployment of smart classrooms, IoT-based systems, and e-governance tools.
>
> o Achieve seamless high-speed internet, LMS integration, smart libraries, paperless work, and health tech adoption.
>
> o Promote AI integration, sustainable transportation, and digital efficiency.

• Hostel Infrastructure Development

> o Evaluate hostel demand and create new facilities across campuses.
>
> o Allocate funds for maintenance and expansion of hostel services to support growing enrolments.

• Distance and Online Learning Expansion

> o Introduce need-based, part-time, and distance learning programs in response to rising GER and digital education demand.

• Establishment of Specialized Centres

> o BGPK – Centre for Indigenous Knowledge: Integrate traditional knowledge with modern learning, foster cultural identity, and promote sustainable innovation.
>
> o CMDS – Centre for Multi-disciplinary Studies: Promote interdisciplinary research and teaching to address complex global issues with innovative solutions.

• International-Level Sports Infrastructure

> o Upgrade sports facilities to host national and international events.
>
> o Create centralized sports management systems and bring in elite athletes for training programs.
>
> o Seek global certifications and establish a sports research unit for future planning and budgeting.

• Establishment of Student-Led Haat Complexes

> o Develop shopping complexes for daily essentials and to showcase student start-up products.
>
> o Encourage entrepreneurship by supporting consumable product development within University and college campuses.

• Environmental and Aesthetic Improvements

> o Add new water bodies within campus to enhance environmental sustainability and visual appeal.

**36.40. Role of Physical Enabler – Long-term Goals**

• Establish a State-of-the-Art Research, Innovation, and Assessment Centre

> o Serve as a policy think tank aligned with national sustainable development goals.
>
> o Drive advanced interdisciplinary research and innovation for societal impact.

• Develop a Central Instrumentation and Supercomputing Facility

> o Provide shared access to high-end scientific equipment and data analysis tools.
>
> o Enable complex simulations, AI training, and large-scale data modeling.

• University Satellite System Initiative

> o Launch a University satellite for research, national security, GPS, remote learning, and global connectivity.
>
> o Partner with organizations like ISRO for satellite deployment and services.

• Lifelong Education and Modular Learning Infrastructure

> o Design flexible, modular programs for upskilling and reskilling throughout life.
>
> o Integrate emerging tech into curriculum design and delivery.

• Competency-Based and Continuous Assessment Infrastructure

> o Create systems to assess student learning via projects, portfolios, peer reviews, and real-time feedback.
>
> o Reduce reliance on traditional, summative examination models.

• Faculty Augmentation for AI Age

> o Establish continuous professional development programs focused on AI, EdTech, and digital pedagogy.
>
> o Equip educators with adaptive learning tools and analytics.

• High-Performance Computing (HPC) and Edge/Cloud Infrastructure

> o Support AI-intensive computations and real-time learning environments.
>
> o Enable scalable research and application development for faculty and students.

• Reliable, High-Speed Internet Infrastructure

> o Ensure uninterrupted connectivity for remote learners and global academic collaborations.
>
> o Provide infrastructure for real-time, synchronous learning experiences.

• Advanced Data Storage and Security Systems

> o Implement secure, scalable data storage with compliance to global data protection standards.
>
> o Ensure rapid recovery and resilience during system failures.

• AI-Driven Infrastructure for Teaching, Learning, and Administration

> o Integrate AI in curriculum delivery, student support systems, admissions, and campus management.
>
> o Leverage predictive analytics for personalized learning and institutional planning.

• Provision of Low-Cost Digital Devices

> o Distribute affordable AI-compatible devices to students and educators.
>
> o Ensure inclusive access to digital learning platforms and smart systems.

• Sustainable Digital Infrastructure

> o Minimize environmental footprint through green computing and carbon-conscious operations.
>
> o Adopt energy-efficient hardware, e-waste management, and eco-friendly data centres.

• Robust Cybersecurity and AI-Specific Risk Management

> o Develop infrastructure to combat AI-based threats and data breaches.
>
> o Ensure system resilience, business continuity, and user trust during digital disruptions.
